Two implementation rules follow and `tasks/T43.md` carries both: **a resolver exception is not an NXDOMAIN** — only NXDOMAIN earns `false`, every other resolver outcome is `null`; and **a NOERROR answer carrying no `Meta:SMP` U record is `null`, not `false`**, because that is the shape a filtering or NXDOMAIN-rewriting middlebox produces and reading it as "not registered" is the 2027 failure in a new costume.

**(d) The TTL is ours alone, the kind is `peppol`, and the NAPTR→SMP binding is never cached. This adds one row to D-045(e)'s table and sharpens D-041(c)/D-043(h).** Both upstreams tell caches not to store — the SMP sends `cache-control: no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, the Directory `no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, proxy-revalidate` — and the NAPTR TTL is **60 seconds**. D-029(e)'s 24 h positive / 1 h negative stands unamended as *our* policy for *our* composed answer, for D-006's reason as D-029(e) restates it, but it is 1,440× the SML's own TTL for the binding and **no upstream endorses it**, so the module docstring must say that the TTL is registry-mcp's policy and that neither upstream publishes one.

* **Kind name `peppol`; key `NO:brreg:peppol:{orgnr}`.** Four segments on D-006's convention, kind third, which is where D-045(e)'s `_ttl_seconds` reads it from — beside `NO:brreg:entity:{orgnr}` and `NO:brreg:filings:{orgnr}`, which `registries/no/client.py` already builds. The second segment names the **module that owns the key**, never the upstream: the upstream here is not brreg, and provenance is carried by `SourceRef`, which is exactly the separation D-041(c) draws. Row: `"peppol": (24 * 3600, 3600)`.
* **One key, for the composed answer.** Never a second key for the NAPTR→SMP binding. A cached binding pins a company to a former SMP for a day against a record whose own TTL is a minute, and (b) has just ruled that the host is data read fresh.
* **A `null` is never cached.** `true` is cached for 24 h; `false` for 1 h through the existing `status="not_found"` path; **`registered: null` is not written to the cache at all**, because it is not an answer — caching "we could not ask" turns a thirty-second outage into an hour of nulls, and D-006's asymmetry exists to shorten wrong answers, not to persist absent ones.
* **Two fetches, one answer, one `SourceRef` — and this is not an exception to D-041(c).** D-041(c) is *one fetch, one `SourceRef`*; D-043(h) sharpened it to one upstream call → one key, with two blocks carrying `SourceRef`s equal in all five fields. Peppol is the third shape: a DNS read and an HTTPS read producing **one** block. The rule that covers all three, and the sentence D-041(c) was missing: **a `SourceRef` describes the fetch that produced the answer, and a discovery step that only says where to ask is not an answer.** The NAPTR fills no field of the block except `smp_url` — so it is disclosed as a *field*, in the caller's hands, rather than hidden or dressed up as a second provenance. `fetched_at` is the moment of the HTTPS read, `source_url` is the URL that returned the content.

**(e) `document_types` carries document type identifiers and nothing else; the block gains exactly one derived field. This adds one field to D-029(b).** `tasks/T48.md` listed `urn:fdc:peppol.eu:2017:poacc:billing:01:1.0` as a `document_types` candidate; it is **not a document type**, it is the *process* identifier (`cenbii-procid-ubl`), it is invisible from the ServiceGroup, and harvesting process ids costs **one extra HTTP request per document type** — 17 more for Equinor. So `document_types` is the full qualified `<scheme>::<value>` strings from the **one** ServiceGroup call, `busdox-docid-qns` or `peppol-doctype-wildcard`. D-029(b)'s `list[str]` is confirmed: usable, not decorative. **No truncation and no cap** — the modal Norwegian participant lists **2** (89.4% of a 500-participant sample; median 2, mean 2.17), Equinor lists 17 and one participant lists 26, so a cap would exist only to bite the long tail, and D-042(j)'s truncation rule applies to histories that grow without bound, which this does not.

**`can_receive_invoice: bool | None` is the sixth field, and it is a table membership, not a judgement.** The statutory question is literally *can this counterparty receive an e-invoice*; the answer is a 155-character URN inside a list of URNs, and a caller who has to re-derive it will do it by substring-matching `billing:3.0`. We hold the table, so we apply it — D-042(j)'s derived-flag rule, exact membership of a **committed table**, never a substring and never a version-range guess. The table is two ids, both from the recon, with DFØ's own equation in the comment (*"Peppol BIS billing v3.0 er det samme som EHF-faktura"*): the BIS Billing 3.0 Invoice id, advertised by **384,834 of 385,268** Norwegian participants (99.89%), and its PINT successor `peppol-doctype-wildcard::…urn:peppol:pint:billing-1::2.1` (3.4%). The CreditNote id is deliberately **not** in the table — the flag is named for the invoice, the credit note is in `document_types` for anyone who needs it, and a flag whose table quietly answers a second question is the retrieval failure D-024(a) is about.

| `registered` / route | `can_receive_invoice` |
|---|---|
| `true`, via the SMP | `True` if a table id is in `document_types`, else `False` |
| `true`, via the Directory | `True` if a table id is present; **`None` otherwise** |
| `false` | `False` — not in the network at all |
| `null` | `None` |

The Directory row is the same asymmetry as (a) and for the same measured reason: Equinor's SMP advertises **17** document types and the Directory lists **12**, the five missing all logistics profiles, and *nothing appeared in the Directory that was not in the SMP*. A Directory list is a subset, so presence is sound and absence is not. That the lag is not universal (GLOBUS AS, `0192:927650487`, matches 2 = 2) is worse for a caller rather than better — you cannot tell a fresh copy from a stale one — and it is why the `None` is not negotiable.

*Declined: process identifiers, `ServiceActivationDate`/`ServiceExpirationDate`, and the AS4 endpoint address.* All three live one HTTP call deeper, per document type, and D-029 asked none of them. The dates are a real caveat on "can receive" — an advertised type may be future-dated or already expired — so they are stated in `notes` rather than used as a filter, because filtering would cost 17 requests to answer a question nobody asked. The AS4 address is a sending access point's business, not a caller's. *Declined: carrying the Directory's `name`, `websites`, `regDate` or `countryCode` onto the block.* They are a second register's opinion of a company we have already described from Enhetsregisteret, and carrying them invites the reconciliation D-018 and D-045(e) both refuse.

**(f) The Directory's `contact` block is never requested, never read, never mapped and never relayed.** The Directory's Privacy Policy: *"the Operators only permit access to personal data in the Directory **by persons who are contractually authorised by at least one of the Operators to use the Peppol Network. These are the only permitted recipients of personal data under this policy.**"* registry-mcp is not a contractually authorised Peppol user. `contact` — the docs describe it as type, name, phone number and email address — is **the only place personal data lives in any of these payloads**; `participantID`, `docTypes`, `name`, `countryCode`, `websites` and `regDate` are company data. So: no model field, no mapper branch that reads it, no `notes` sentence that could carry one, and the Directory's `contact=` query parameter is never sent. D-028's four privacy preconditions are not satisfied and this is not the block that satisfies them (D-042(e)). **The SML and SMP path carries no personal data at all** — which is one more reason it is primary, and it is the sentence to reach for the next time a route choice looks like a tie.

**(g) `SourceRef.license` carries a stated absence — not `None`, and not a licence name. D-029(a)'s supporting quote is replaced. One `HUMAN_TODO.md` item, non-blocking.** Nobody publishes a licence for any of it: Digdir's ELMA docs state no terms, no rate limit and no licence for the lookup path; OpenPeppol states none for the SML or for an SMP; the Directory states none. data.norge.no catalogues *"Mottakere i ELMA"*, publisher Digitaliseringsdirektoratet, access level *"Allmenn tilgang"*, **Lisens: "Ikke oppgitt"** (not stated). Digdir's house recommendation for *new* open datasets is CC BY 4.0 or CC0 — a recommendation Digdir has not applied here, and quoting it would be the error `tasks/T26-recon.md` refused with Bolagsverket.

The value is a sentence in D-038's shape, and the difference from Sweden is the point. Bolagsverket **grants** reuse in prose and merely has no name for it, so D-038's string states the permission and then says there is no name. Here there is no name *and no grant* — the data is published to the open internet, ungated, as the network's own designed access path, and no one has said anything about reuse — so the string must record the absence without inventing a permission:

> `license = "Not stated — neither OpenPeppol nor Digitaliseringsdirektoratet publishes a licence for the Peppol SML, for any SMP or for the Peppol Directory; data.norge.no records the ELMA dataset's licence as 'Ikke oppgitt' (not stated)"`

*Declined: `None`, which `tasks/T49.md` suggested.* `SourceRef.license` is `str | None` and every field on that model defaults to `None`, so `None` already reads as *nobody filled this in*; a **stated** absence is a fact and D-038 refused an empty field for exactly this reason — *"'we don't know' is not what is true here"*. Two states in one field, one of which is our own omission, is D-011. *Declined: putting the absence only in `notes`.* Provenance is the product; a reader who checks the licence field must not have to read a note to learn that the field is empty on purpose. **`SourceRef.license = None` is now reserved for "not recorded" everywhere, and never means "the publisher states none".**

**`HUMAN_TODO.md` gets one item, and it blocks nothing.** It is one e-mail to Digdir asking under what terms ELMA participant data may be relayed, and its answer would shorten a string and nothing else — so it must be marked non-blocking, or it will read as a gate on a 1 January 2027 feature. It is worth asking anyway for a reason worth writing down: Digdir can only answer **for ELMA**, and (b) has just established that ~2% of Norwegian participants answer from another SMP entirely, so even a perfect reply narrows the sentence rather than replacing it.

**D-029(a)'s quote no longer exists and must not be repeated.** D-029(a) rests its "the two registers can disagree" argument on DFØ conceding *"ELMA-registeret har ikke oppdatert informasjonen fra adresseregisteret"*. That sentence is **gone** from the DFØ page as revised 14 August 2026 (0 hits in the live HTML). Its **conclusion survives and is better evidenced than before** — by the measured 17-vs-12 Equinor diff in (e), by the page's current sentence, which describes a lag in the other direction (*"Peppol Directory har ikke registret eller endret informasjon tilsendt fra adresseregister (SMP eller kjent som ELMA i Norge)"*), and by the standing fact that ELMA and Enhetsregisteret are different registers under different operators. Only the citation changes. **The retired sentence must not appear in `notes`, in `rules_markdown()`, in a spec or in a brief**; `grep -rn "oppdatert informasjonen" src/ *.md` should return this file and `tasks/T48-recon.md` and nothing else.

**(h) Sequencing: `peppol` is a country declaration, not a universal one, and T43 runs in round 3 beside T44 and T47.** Peppol is Norway-only here and the reason is not that the network is Norwegian — it is not — but that **everything we verified is**. D-045(e) put `lei` on the base class because GLEIF publishes every jurisdiction *from one endpoint, under one licence, with one TTL*; not one of those three premises holds here. The participant identifier needs a country's own ISO 6523 ICD, and `0192:` is Norway's — the recon established no other country's; the answer's provenance is a **different SMP per participant**, not one endpoint; the licence sentence in (g) was earned by reading a Norwegian catalogue page. And Sweden could not have it in any case: the SMP GET puts the identifier in a URL path to a third-party host, which is D-045(e)'s own exclusion rule for `id_may_be_personal`. So `BrregRegistry.supported_includes` gains `"peppol"`, `universal_includes` is untouched, and `include=["peppol"]` on GB or SE is `bad_request` naming that country's allowed set — never a silently ignored value and never an empty block (D-042(d)(3)). Extending it to a fourth country is a later entry that has read that country's ICD and terms, and it is named here so it is a decision rather than a gap.

**T43 cannot share round 2** — it needs D-045(e)'s TTL table to exist (T42) and `registries/no/__init__.py` is held by T38. **It goes in round 3, beside T44 and T47, and the three are disjoint**: T44 holds `core/registry.py`, `mcp/server.py`, `api/main.py` and `registries/se/`; T47 holds only the `Charge`/`ChargeBlock` class bodies in `core/models.py`, `registries/gb/`, `legal/terms.md` and `UK_SPEC.md`; T43 holds the tail of `core/models.py` (a new class immediately before `CompanyReport`, one field after `financials`, one `__all__` line), `core/cache.py`'s table, all of `registries/no/`, and `pyproject.toml`'s `dependencies` list plus the regenerated `uv.lock`. Nothing touches `mcp/server.py`, `api/main.py` or the server card — T46 does. **Two coordination costs, both named so neither is discovered late.** T46's prose list **grows by `peppol`**: the words *Peppol*, *ELMA*, *e-invoice*, *EHF* and *2027* must reach `lookup_company`'s description text under D-042(d)(2), and T46 is not yet dispatched, so this is a sentence added to a brief rather than a second surfaces-sync round. And T44's `DEADLINE_INCLUDES` **does not gain `peppol`**: D-029(f) refuses to emit a `Deadline` for the 2027 duty, and D-043(j) restricts `company_deadlines` to include values that can change a date.
Reason: three of D-045(f)'s four facts came back exactly as D-029 imagined them, and the fourth came back as the opposite of a sentence D-029 had already written down — which is the whole case for reading the wire before briefing rather than after. The correction is small on the page and large in effect: `registered: false` was one composed inference away from being emitted for a company that is registered, on the one feature in this repository with a criminal statute behind it, and the composition happened between two parts of one entry that were each true. The other thing worth keeping is (c). The dependency question looked like a matter of taste and was not: the substitute that avoids the dependency puts an unrelated third party into the trust path and the access log of a legal answer, loses the locked-down case it was supposed to win, and would have to be disclosed in a provenance field to be honest — and the moment you write that disclosure sentence out, the choice makes itself. That is the second time in two entries (D-045(b), (d)) that a source's or a mechanism's own terms decided something a ranking had already scored.
Applies to tasks: **`tasks/T43.md`** (R-6, this entry) and `CORE_ROADMAP_SPEC.md` §8, updated by this task; **`tasks/T46.md`** gains `peppol` in the prose list (h); T44 and T47 unchanged except that T44's `DEADLINE_INCLUDES` must not carry `peppol`; **amends D-029(a)** (the supporting quote is replaced; the conclusion stands), **D-029(b)** (`false` only via SML/SMP; `can_receive_invoice` is a sixth field), **D-029(d)** (the route sentence becomes route *and SMP*, derived never constant; the Directory fallback is positive-only and is consulted only on failure) and **D-029(e)** (the TTL is confirmed as ours, a `null` is never cached); **sharpens D-041(c) and D-043(h)** (a `SourceRef` describes the fetch that produced the answer; a discovery step is disclosed as a field); **adds the `peppol` row** to D-045(e)'s per-kind TTL table; **reserves `SourceRef.license = None` for "not recorded"** (g); R-6 is unblocked and `CORE_ROADMAP_SPEC.md` §2's Peppol row is closed; **D-024, D-028 and D-031 unamended, `universal_includes` untouched, and the published tool count does not move**

### D-047 — Corporate parents are a second include value, not a wider `lei`: the state is free, the exception *word* is the whole of what is relayed, and four of the brief's six fields are struck on what the wire showed; the FCA is briefed behind a VERIFY gate because its endpoint list is behind its registration; UK filed-document links are refused; HMRC VAT's block is ruled before its credential exists; and Sweden's financial figures are unblocked by measurement while Britain's are closed by the register's own population
Date: 2026-09-09
Decision: rules `~/registry-mcp/research/07-product-improvements.md` item 10's group walk for GLEIF Level 2, item 15's document links, and the two sources D-045(c) and D-045(h) deferred on sequencing rather than merit, and closes `tasks/T52-recon.md`'s two open questions. Seven parts — (f) and (g) were added on 2026-09-09 after the recon landed on `main` at `a2faccd`. It **amends D-045(e)** by name (`universal_includes` gains a second member; the per-kind TTL table gains a row), **sharpens D-042(c)** (the distinct-question rule applied to a block, with the source's own vocabulary as the evidence) and **D-046(d)** (a fan-out of fetches producing one block), and **binds D-028(1) to a third-party field for the second time** (D-042(e)(3) was the first) It also **amends D-043(i)** and **reverses D-041(g) for Sweden** (f), and **adds two rows** to D-045(e)'s per-kind TTL table. D-024, D-031 and the published tool count are unamended, and **D-028 is not amended — every ruling below stays on the company side of its line**.

**Verified live and keyless on 2026-09-09**, and every number below that is not from a committed fixture comes from one of these. **GLEIF** (`api.gleif.org`, ~140 requests): 8 named entities resolved through `filter[entity.registeredAs]`; the Level 1 `relationships` block of **1,800** records (600 each for NO, GB, SE, at `page[size]=200`); **117** reporting exceptions fetched individually (57 NO, 60 GB); **31** `direct-parent-relationship` records; 30 `SOLE_PROPRIETOR` records and their `direct-children` counts; and the `?include=` allow-list probed one path at a time. **GLEIF's own documentation** (`www.gleif.org`): the Reporting Exceptions 2.1 format page, the Level 2 "who owns whom" page, and `meta/lei-data-terms-of-use`. **HMRC** (`developer.service.hmrc.gov.uk`): the *Check a UK VAT number* v2.0 page and its OpenAPI 3.0.3 description document. **The FCA** (`www.fca.org.uk`, `register.fca.org.uk`): the Financial Services Register page, the privacy page for the Register services, and the *Register Extract Service Subscribers' Handbook*, April 2026, 59 pp. **Finanstilsynet** (`www.finanstilsynet.no`): the open-API page for *verksemdsregisteret*. **Not called, and nothing below rests on an unverified claim about them:** `api.foretak.dev`, Companies House, Bolagsverket, the Companies House Document API, the FCA Register API itself, and HMRC's API. `833286602` and `833285602` were not looked up.

**(a) Corporate parents are ruled in as `include=["parents"]` — a second include value with its own block, not a widening of `lei`. Two of the brief's fields are free, two are bought, and two are struck.**

**The fact the whole ruling turns on: the *state* is already in the body we fetch today.** Every Level 1 record carries a `relationships` object, and its `direct-parent` and `ultimate-parent` members expose **different link keys in the two cases** — `reporting-exception` when no parent is reported, `lei-record` + `relationship-record` when one is. Classified over 1,800 records without a single extra request:

| | records sampled | direct parent disclosed | reporting exception | neither |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NO | 600 | **19** (3.2%) | 581 | 0 |
| GB | 600 | **12** (2.0%) | 588 | 0 |
| SE | 600 | **9** (1.5%) | 591 | 0 |

So *"does GLEIF hold a disclosed corporate parent for this entity"* is answerable from the search response `core/gleif.py:fetch_lei` already makes, at zero marginal upstream cost, and the two sides can disagree in kind — the SE sample has **9 direct-parent disclosures against 10 ultimate**, so one record reports an ultimate parent and excepts its direct one. `direct` and `ultimate` are modelled independently and are never collapsed.

**It is a second include value and not a wider `LeiRecord`, and the first of the four reasons is the source's own.** *Declined: `LeiRecord` gains `direct_parent` / `ultimate_parent` / `parent_reporting_exception`,* which is the shape `tasks/T51.md` proposed.

1. **GLEIF makes D-042(c)'s argument for us, in its own vocabulary.** Level 1 is published as *"who is who"* and Level 2 as *"who owns whom"* — two named products, two formats (LEI-CDF 3.1, RR-CDF 2.1 and Reporting Exceptions 2.1), two endpoints. D-042(c)'s rule one level down is *a block is bought by a distinct question*, and here the publisher has already split the questions and named them. Nothing in this file has had that evidence before; every previous application of the rule was ours.
2. **D-011, for the sixth time in this file.** `LeiRecord.lei = None` **inside a present block** already means *GLEIF holds no LEI for this entity* (D-026(c)). A parent field on the same model is `None` for three reasons — not asked, no LEI at all, LEI exists but no parent disclosed — and the third is 97% of the population.
3. **Cost, and D-045(e) is the one it would break.** `include=["lei"]` is **one** request and D-045(e) chose a 7-day TTL partly *"to keep a courtesy load off a free service"*. Folding parents into `lei` triples that load for every caller who wanted only the identifier.
4. **A fourth state that only a separate block can express.** `include=["parents"]` on an entity GLEIF holds **no LEI** for is a **present** block with both sides `None` and a note saying so — GLEIF cannot hold Level 2 for an entity it has no Level 1 for. On a widened `LeiRecord` that state is indistinguishable from "has an LEI, no parent".

**The name is `parents`.** *Declined: `group`.* It promises the whole tree — GLEIF publishes `direct-children` and `ultimate-children` too, and this block fetches neither — and it collides with `CompanyReport.in_group`, which is Enhetsregisteret's `erIKonsern`, a different register's answer to a neighbouring question; two shapes for one concept is what D-042(b) declined by name. *Declined: `ownership`, and this is the important one.* GLEIF's relationship type on the wire is **`IS_DIRECTLY_CONSOLIDATED_BY`** / `IS_ULTIMATELY_CONSOLIDATED_BY`, and GLEIF's own definition of the reported relationship is the **accounting consolidating parent**, not the owner. A block named `ownership` that returns accounting consolidation is a retrieval key that means one thing and returns another (D-024(a)) *and* a two-states-in-one-field collapse (D-011) in a single word. The field descriptions must carry the distinction in terms: **a parent here is the entity that consolidates this entity's accounts, which is not the same as its majority shareholder, and neither implies the other.** *Declined: `parent` singular* — there are two sides and they can differ.

**The cost, measured rather than estimated. `tasks/T51.md`'s "two more GLEIF requests per lookup" is right for 97% of lookups and wrong for the rest; the honest number is a bounded fan-out.** The rule is **one extra request per side, and the endpoint is chosen by the link key the first response already returned**:

* link key `reporting-exception` → `GET …/{lei}/{direct|ultimate}-parent-reporting-exception` → the `category` and the `reason`.
* link key `lei-record` → `GET …/{lei}/{direct|ultimate}-parent` → the parent's own Level 1 record: `lei`, `entity.legalName.name`, `entity.jurisdiction`, `registration.status`. **And** `GET …/{lei}/{direct|ultimate}-parent-relationship` → the corroboration (see below).

So **2 extra requests on the 97% path, 4 on the 3% path, expected 2.06, worst case bounded at 4**, all of them concurrent under `lookup_with`'s existing bounded concurrency (D-042(b)), so the latency is one round trip and not four. *Declined: `?include=direct-parent,ultimate-parent` on the search request as a cheaper route.* GLEIF supports JSON:API compound documents on an allow-list of exactly those two paths — every other relationship path is a 400 — **and its `included` array carries resource identifier objects only, `{"type", "id"}`, with no `attributes`**. Measured on `98450073EGD581D89F03`: `included` is present, `included[0]` has no `attributes`, and the body grows from 3,528 to 4,573 bytes (+30%) on the exception path to carry nothing at all. **This is the single thing an implementer will get wrong**: `include=` looks like it fetches the parent, the response looks like a compound document, the code compiles, and `legal_name` is `null` on every record forever. It buys exactly one thing — the parent's LEI on the 3% path, which the `/…-parent` fetch returns anyway — and it is not used.

**The shape.** `core/models.py` gains two models; `CompanyReport` gains one field, `parents: ParentBlock | None = None`, `null` on every lookup that did not ask.

```python
class ParentLink(_Base):
    lei: str | None = None
    legal_name: str | None = None
    jurisdiction: str | None = None
    registration_status: str | None = None
    corroboration_level: str | None = None
    reporting_exception: str | None = None
    source_url: str | None = None

class ParentBlock(_Base):
    direct: ParentLink | None = None
    ultimate: ParentLink | None = None
    provenance: SourceRef
    notes: list[str] = []
```

Within one `ParentLink`, **`lei` and `reporting_exception` are mutually exclusive and at least one is populated** — that is the two-states discipline, enforced by a validator and asserted in a test, not left as a convention. `direct`/`ultimate` being `None` means GLEIF publishes neither for that side, which was **0 of 1,800** and is therefore modelled but not expected; the note says so.

**The `NATURAL_PERSONS` rule, which is the line D-028 draws and the reason this block is buildable at all.** GLEIF's `ExceptionReasonEnum` is a **closed list of code strings**, published in the Reporting Exceptions 2.1 format: `NO_LEI`, `NATURAL_PERSONS`, `NON_CONSOLIDATING`, `NO_KNOWN_PERSON`, `NON_PUBLIC`, plus five retained-but-deprecated-since-2022-03-01 (`BINDING_LEGAL_COMMITMENTS`, `LEGAL_OBSTACLES`, `DISCLOSURE_DETRIMENTAL`, `DETRIMENT_NOT_EXCLUDED`, `CONSENT_NOT_OBTAINED`). GLEIF's own gloss of the first group: *"the entity is controlled by a natural person(s) without any intermediate legal entity (**"Natural Person(s)"**); the entity is controlled by legal entities not subject to consolidation (**"Non-Consolidating"**); and where there is no known person(s) controlling the entity (**"No Known Person"** e.g.; the entity is controlled by diverse shareholders)."*

> **`NATURAL_PERSONS` is a category word from a closed enumeration, not a name. Relaying the word is company data — it says a person exists and nothing else about them, which is what a structured "we cannot tell you" looks like (D-028(6), D-011). Relaying anything beyond it is not company data and is barred by D-028 whether or not GLEIF ever publishes it: no name, no count, no nationality, no residence, no date, no derived flag such as `family_owned` or `individually_controlled`, and never a lookup key.**

**And the word is unreliable in a way that must ship beside it, because the wire is emphatic about that.** Of 117 ultimate-parent exceptions fetched individually, **49 of 57 Norwegian ones (86%) and 28 of 60 British ones (47%) read `NATURAL_PERSONS`** — so the modal thing this block would say about a Norwegian company is *"controlled by one or more natural persons"*. Now the four largest entities in the sample of named companies, all four of them listed companies sitting at the top of their own consolidated group, in materially identical positions:

| Entity | LEI | `reason` |
|---|---|---|
| **EQUINOR ASA** (67% owned by the Norwegian State) | `OW6OFBNCKXC4US5C7523` | **`NATURAL_PERSONS`** |
| DNB BANK ASA | `549300GKFG0RYRRQ1414` | `NON_CONSOLIDATING` |
| TELENOR ASA | `549300IM1QSBY4SLPM26` | `NON_CONSOLIDATING` |
| TESCO PLC | `2138002P5RNKC5W2JZ46` | `NON_CONSOLIDATING` |
| BP P.L.C. | `213800LH1BZH3DI6G760` | `NO_KNOWN_PERSON` |
| Telefonaktiebolaget LM Ericsson | `549300W9JLPW15XIFM52` | `NO_KNOWN_PERSON` |
| CARILLION PLC | `6SNZTEXLR1M5211YYB89` | `NO_KNOWN_PERSON` |

**Three different words for one situation, and the one on Norway's state-controlled oil company asserts, in GLEIF's own definition, that it is controlled by natural persons without any intermediate legal entity.** That is false, it is the most-cited company in this repository, and it is the first record anyone will test against. The reason is filer-supplied — GLEIF's own text calls it *"a single reason provided by the legal entity"* — and nothing corroborates it. So the field's description and the block's `notes` must say, in terms: **this word is the entity's own stated reason for not reporting a parent, it is not verified by GLEIF or by us, the four reasons are not applied consistently between filers, and `NATURAL_PERSONS` on a company with a known institutional owner is a filing artefact rather than a fact about its owners.** *Declined: suppressing or normalising `NATURAL_PERSONS`.* Suppression would leave the 86% case as an unexplained empty block, which is D-011 in the other direction; normalising it would be us overwriting a source, which is D-018's whole finding. Relay it, and relay what is true about it.

**`reference` is never carried, and this is D-042(e)'s free-text rule arriving for the third time.** The format defines `ExceptionReference` as `[1,*]` of `Tokenized500Type` — up to 500 characters each, *"References of the law, regulation or other element of the legal framework to support reason(s) provided by the legal entity"*. It was `null` on **117 of 117** exceptions fetched, so nobody here has ever read a populated one. D-042(e)(1) found personal particulars inside a third party's free text twice, and D-045(a) applied the same treatment to two charge fields; a filer's free-hand legal justification for withholding a parent — a field whose whole purpose is to explain an opt-out — is the most likely place in this payload for a person to be named. **No model field, no mapper branch that reads it, no `notes` sentence that could carry it.** It may be reconsidered by a later entry that has read a populated one; it cannot be reconsidered by an implementer.

**Two of the brief's fields are struck on measurement, and one it did not ask for is bought.** `tasks/T51.md` asked for *"the relationship's status/period as GLEIF publishes them"*. Over the **31** disclosed direct-parent relationships in the sample:

* **`relationship.status` is `ACTIVE` on 31 of 31, and the relationship record's own `registration.status` is `PUBLISHED` on 31 of 31.** These endpoints return the *current* relationship, so both fields are constants on the wire. D-043(d) struck `oppstillingsplan` for exactly this — *"a field that is permanently `None` asserts nothing"* — and a field that is permanently the same non-`None` value asserts nothing either. **Struck.**
* **`relationship.periods` is a list of typed intervals with an open vocabulary** — `ACCOUNTING_PERIOD` (31), `RELATIONSHIP_PERIOD` (31) and `DOCUMENT_FILING_PERIOD` (2) were observed, three meanings under one key. Modelling it needs its own model and its own entry, and the `ACCOUNTING_PERIOD` is a financial-statements concept wearing a relationship costume — D-041(g)'s bar, mirrored. **Struck**, with the RELATIONSHIP_PERIOD start date named in `notes` as the one thing a caller loses.
* **`registration.corroborationLevel` is carried, and it is the field that earns the second request on the 3% path.** It varies three ways over 31 records — `PARTIALLY_CORROBORATED` 11, `ENTITY_SUPPLIED_ONLY` 10, `FULLY_CORROBORATED` 10 — so **roughly a third of all disclosed parent links are the filer's own assertion that nobody has checked** (Equinor Energy AS → Equinor ASA is one of them; NatWest Markets plc → NatWest Group plc is `FULLY_CORROBORATED`). Shipping a parent link without it would present those two with equal confidence, which is the one thing this product exists not to do (D-018, R-5's whole purpose). National-vocabulary-in-values, verbatim, never folded into a boolean (D-025(d)).

**A parent's `legal_name` can be a natural person's name, the wire says so, and the rule that follows is D-042(e)(3)'s.** `tasks/T51.md` states that GLEIF Level 2 *"names legal entities only"*. That is true of the graph — every node is an LEI and GLEIF issues LEIs to legal entities — and it is **not** a guarantee about the string in `legalName`: **126,936 LEI records carry `entity.category: SOLE_PROPRIETOR`**, and the first three returned are `Chander Shekhar arora`, `M/s DR ARUNISHA ENT HOSPITAL` and `AKASH GARG`. None of 30 sampled sole proprietors had a single direct child, so a sole-proprietor *parent* was **not observed** and is presumably rare — but nothing in RR-CDF 2.1 forbids it, and "presumably rare" is not a field-level guarantee. So **`ParentLink.legal_name` is bound by D-028(1) exactly as `Charge.parties_entitled` is (D-042(e)(3), as sharpened by D-045(a))**: it is never a lookup key, never an index, never searchable, never reaches a log (D-040), and `legal/terms.md`'s sentence naming the fields where a natural person's name can reach a response gains this one — the fourth. **`entity.category` is not carried** on the parent (it would be a claim about a third entity we did not look up, and its useful case is the one D-028 covers anyway), but the implementer must know it exists, which is why it is written here.

**The mechanism, the cache and the one implementation hazard.** `parents` joins `lei` in `Registry.universal_includes` — `frozenset({"lei", "parents"})`, **amending D-045(e)** — for D-045(e)'s three reasons unchanged: GLEIF publishes every jurisdiction from one endpoint, under one licence, with one TTL, so a per-country declaration would be three modules asserting one global fact. Sweden therefore does not get it either, automatically, through `effective_includes`' `id_may_be_personal` subtraction, and for the same reason: the identifier goes to a third-party host in a URL query string (D-039's exposure category). `include=["parents"]` for `SE` is `bad_request` naming SE's allowed set, never an empty block (D-042(d)(3)).

* **Cache kind `parents`; key `{COUNTRY}:{registry}:parents:{id}`; row `"parents": (7 * 24 * 3600, 24 * 3600)`** in `core/cache.py`'s table. Same pair as `lei` and for D-045(e)'s reasons unchanged: a relationship record renews annually (the NatWest record read `nextRenewalDate: 2027-08-18` against a `lastUpdateDate` of 2026-07-26), nothing statutory or credit-bearing turns on the answer, and the harm from re-asking a free service points at the free service. **One key for the whole fan-out**, holding the composed answer, so the two sides never age apart — D-043(h)(1)'s "one key per upstream call" generalised to one key per composed answer, which is what D-046(d) already did for Peppol's two-route fetch.
* **The discovery request is shared, never made twice. This is the hazard.** `lookup_with` runs attachment methods concurrently, so `Registry.lei()` and `Registry.parents()` will both want the same `lei-records` search on a cold cache and will **race and make two requests** — the identical trap D-043(h)(3) named for `filings`/`financials` and the reason that ruling exists. `core/gleif.py` already caches the raw search body under `{COUNTRY}:{registry}:lei:{id}`; the parents path **reads that entry and populates it when cold, through one shared in-flight result**, and never issues its own search. A lookup asking for both makes **one** search request, and `tasks/T53.md` asserts the count rather than assuming it.
* **One `SourceRef` for the block, and the discovery step is disclosed as a field, not as provenance.** D-046(d)'s sentence governs: *a `SourceRef` describes the fetch that produced the answer, and a discovery step that only says where to ask is not an answer.* The search fills no field of `ParentBlock`; the two-to-four relationship fetches fill all of them. So `provenance.fetched_at` is the moment the fan-out completed, `provenance.source_url` is `https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records/{lei}` — the one record every link in the fan-out hangs off — `provenance.cached` describes the `parents` entry, and each side's own `ParentLink.source_url` carries the exact URL that returned it. That is the same disclosure `smp_url` gets in D-046(d), for the same reason.
* **`license` is `"CC0 1.0"`, unchanged, and it is checked rather than assumed.** GLEIF's Terms of Use define *LEI Reference Data* as *"the information available in the Global LEI Repository in relation to an LEI"* whose categories *"are listed on Common Data File Format"* — which is where RR-CDF 2.1 and Reporting Exceptions 2.1 are published — and state that *"The data available through the Access Service are provided under the CC0 licence"*. Level 2 is inside the same grant as Level 1. D-026(c)'s anti-impersonation note is unchanged and applies here too.

**`tasks/T53.md` is written** for a Sonnet: core-only footprint, the endpoints and the two link keys pinned to the strings above, the four struck fields named as struck, and the mutual exclusion tested as a contract.

**(b) The FCA is ruled in and briefed, the include value is `regulator`, and the brief opens with a VERIFY gate — because the FCA publishes its endpoint list only to registered users, and D-045(c) refused to write an unverified endpoint into a brief.**

**What is now primary-sourced, and it settles two of D-045(c)'s three `[search]` marks.** The *Register Extract Service Subscribers' Handbook*, FCA, April 2026, §1.13–1.19, verbatim: *"The FCA offers a free API (currently in **BETA**) to access data from the FS Register programmatically. **This is a separate service to the RES**."* · *"You can register for the API at `https://register.fca.org.uk/Developer/s/` by clicking on the Register to be a New User link."* · *"Registering as an API user will give you access to the API portal **which provides more detail on what is available via the API, and the available endpoints**."* · *"The Register API is designed for individual look-ups rather than bulk data access. It currently supports queries for one entity at a time and is subject to rate limits."* · *"**The current rate limit is 50 requests per 10 seconds.**"* · *"We do not offer any 'premium' versions of the API, nor the ability to raise the limits referred to above."* The FS Register page adds: *"currently free of charge, allows users to generate a unique key"*, and *"As the API is a free service, we do not offer any service-level agreements (SLAs) or guarantees for uptime or issue resolution."*

So the 50-per-10-seconds figure is no longer `[search]`, and neither is the self-serve key. **What is still unread is unreadable without the registration**: §1.15 says the endpoint list lives *behind* the portal, and the API's own licence terms are accepted there. This is why part (d)(1) exists and why the brief is shaped the way it is.

**Do not quote the RES licence at the API.** The handbook's §2.12 and §2.15 — *"You are not permitted to share or re-sell the data under this licence. This includes sharing the data with any other legal entity, including subsidiaries, parent companies, or any other organisation within the same corporate group"* — read exactly like a D-045(b) refusal and would end this part in one line. They are **not applicable**: they are the fee bands of the paid Register Extract Service, a service §1.13 says in terms is separate from the API, delivered by a third party (Spectrum Data Management Limited) under a subscriber agreement we are not party to. Quoting them at the API would be the conflation D-045(b) and (d) exist to prevent, in reverse — refusing a source on a neighbouring document's terms rather than its own. **The API's own terms have not been read by anyone here, and until they are, `SourceRef.license` for this block cannot be written.** That is D-038's and D-046(g)'s discipline: the string states what the publisher states, and neither an invented permission nor an inherited prohibition.

**The include value is `regulator`.** *Declined: `fca`.* National vocabulary in a field name is D-004, and the second filler already exists: **Finanstilsynet's *virksomhetsregister*** — the register D-045(h) and `~/mcp-growth/DEPTH.md` do not mention at all, and which `~/research/registry-mcp/` does not cover either (checked: Finanstilsynet appears there only as the author of AML guidance and of fraud statistics, never as a register). It was renamed from *Konsesjonsregisteret* in 2020, it is updated hourly, and Finanstilsynet publishes an **open API** for it at `https://api.finanstilsynet.no/registry/` with an OpenAPI 3.0 description: *"Finanstilsynet sitt opne API for verksemdsregisteret gir informasjon om juridiske einingar (**personar eller selskap**) med lisensar som blir nytta ved drift i Noreg"*, paging capped at 200 pages × 50 results. So the same block has a keyless Norwegian filler within reach, on a country where we have the most users, and a name that says `fca` would have to be renamed the day it lands. **Its parenthesis is also a warning: *personar eller selskap*. The firm-only rule below is not an FCA rule, it is the block's rule.**

*Declined: `authorisation`.* It is the best description of the answer and it is unsafe as a retrieval key for two reasons D-024(a) is about. It has a national spelling variant — a caller writing `authorization` gets a `bad_request` — and **every other value in the set (`charges`, `filings`, `financials`, `insolvency`, `lei`, `peppol`) is spelling-stable**, which is not a coincidence for a string an agent types from memory. And it sits in a schema beside `api_key_env` and `include_api_key_env`, where "authorisation" already means something else. *Declined: `licence`/`license`* — the same spelling problem, plus `SourceRef.license` already owns the word. **`regulator` names a source category the way `filings` and `charges` name a record category**, and the *question* is carried where D-042(d)(2) puts it: the words *FCA*, *authorised*, *regulated*, *permissions* and *appointed representative* must reach `lookup_company`'s description text, which is the surfaces-sync task's job and not the implementer's.

**The block, and it is firm-only by D-028 regardless of what the licence turns out to permit.** Firm reference number; authorisation status; a permissions summary; appointed-representative relationships **as firm records only** (principal and AR both being firms, each with its own FRN, so the block is a list of company↔company links exactly like `ParentBlock` and never a person); and the date the register last changed the record. **No individuals, no approved persons, no Directory of certified and assessed persons, no controlled functions, no disciplinary history** — D-045(c) already ruled this and D-028(1) is why: an individual endpoint on this register is a person→firms index, which is the one shape D-028(1) bars outright. The FCA's own privacy page confirms the register *"feature[s] data about individuals carrying out specific roles"*, so the barred surface is real and adjacent, not hypothetical.

**The two-credential expression, D-045(c)'s mechanism, built here.** `Registry.include_api_key_env: ClassVar[Mapping[str, str]] = {}` mapping include value → environment variable name, published on `CountryInfo`, so `list_countries` answers "the base lookup needs `COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY`; the `regulator` attachment needs `FCA_API_KEY` as well" in one round trip. `requires_api_key` and `api_key_env` keep meaning the base lookup. An attachment whose variable is unset raises `upstream_error` with a hint naming **its own** variable; the block goes absent and the lookup still succeeds (D-042(b)). Unchanged from D-045(c) and restated only because T54 is the task that builds it.

**The VERIFY gate, and it is the load-bearing part of the brief.** D-045(c) refused to rule the FCA in because doing so *"would mean writing an unverified endpoint into a brief, which is the one thing D-043's live sweep was worth doing to avoid"*. That objection has not been answered — the endpoint list is behind the registration — so it is **converted into a gate rather than waived**, on `tasks/T44.md`'s precedent where no non-December Swedish date is computed until Bolagsverket's own table is cited. **`tasks/T54.md` Part 0 is a recon: with Kim's key in hand, record the base URL, the exact auth header names, the firm endpoints' real response envelopes as committed fixtures, the portal's licence text as the literal `SourceRef.license` string, and whatever rate-limit headers the service returns. Nothing is written into `src/` until Part 0 is committed, and if the licence text forbids relaying, the task stops there and reports** — one paragraph in this file closes it, exactly as D-045(b) closed VIES. The brief carries the field list, the naming, the mechanism and the done-check so that Part 0 is a morning and not a redesign.

**(c) UK filed documents as links: no. `FiledDocument` does not gain a `document_url`, and there is no `resource_link`.** Three reasons and the first is sufficient. **A URL that needs a credential the caller does not have is not a link, it is a disappointment with a scheme on the front.** `links.document_metadata` points at `document-api.company-information.service.gov.uk`, which requires the same Companies House key — the operator's key, held by the server, never the caller's. Relaying it means one of two things: the caller cannot fetch it, which is a field whose only content is a 401 they will discover for themselves; or we proxy the bytes, which is a second host, a second `SourceRef`, iXBRL or PDF payloads, and the project `research/07-product-improvements.md` item 15 itself sizes at **M** — and which D-045(h) already ruled out in one sentence (*"it needs filing history first, returns binary, and iXBRL parsing is a project, which is D-041(g)'s bar in a second country"*). Nothing about that has changed in a day. **Second, `document_id` already carries the whole of the honest value.** `FiledDocument.document_id` relays Companies House's `transaction_id` verbatim, and its description already says the document *"lives behind a separate host, which is a second upstream with its own provenance and out of scope for this block (D-041(c))"* and that it *"is the key a support case with the register can name"* — a caller with their own key builds the URL from that handle in one line, and a caller without one is told plainly why they cannot. A `document_url` beside it adds a second representation of one fact, which is D-004's "one shape" argument at field level. **Third, `resource_link` is the wrong vehicle even if the first two fell.** D-042(b) declined MCP resources for attachments by name — *"a resource is pull-by-URI, most clients never enumerate them for a model"* — and a `resource_link` whose URI the client cannot dereference is that objection plus a broken promise. This is **decoration**, it is refused, and the one thing that would reopen it is Companies House publishing a keyless or caller-scoped document URL, which is a change on their side and not a decision on ours.

**(d) The registrations only Kim can make: `HUMAN_TODO.md` §7.10, three items, in Kim's order.** Written this task. (1) the FCA developer-portal registration, which unblocks T54's Part 0 and nothing else; (2) HMRC's developer-hub registration, which is two weeks of somebody else's clock and therefore starts first if both are wanted; (3) the D-028(4) lawyer step, which is not a registration and is on the list because the research names officers as the highest-demand real gap and because D-028 has stood blocked on it since 2026-09-05. **Every key is stored under `~/secrets/registry-mcp/`, never in the repo, and no agent on this project ever writes one anywhere.** The env var names T54 and any HMRC brief will read are fixed here so they cannot drift: **`FCA_API_KEY`**, and — see (e) — **`HMRC_VAT_CLIENT_ID`** and **`HMRC_VAT_CLIENT_SECRET`**.

**(e) HMRC's *Check a UK VAT number* v2: the block is ruled before the credential exists, the unverified route only, and the GB sole trader is the whole of the question.**

**What the wire and the documentation say, read on 2026-09-09.** Version 2 is `2.0 - beta`, last updated 1 April 2026; production `https://api.service.hmrc.gov.uk`, sandbox `https://test-api.service.hmrc.gov.uk`. *"Version 2 moved the Check a UK VAT Number API behind authentication. We moved the API behind authentication so we can fully understand our users."* · *"Registering should take around 2 weeks. It may take longer if we need more information. You will get production credentials once you have tested in the Sandbox environment and accepted the Terms of Use 2.0."* The OpenAPI 3.0.3 description gives the rest: security scheme `applicationRestricted`, **OAuth 2.0 client-credentials**, token URL `https://api.service.hmrc.gov.uk/oauth/token`, scope `read:vat`, `Accept: application/vnd.hmrc.2.0+json` required. Two operations: `GET /organisations/vat/check-vat-number/lookup/{targetVrn}` returning `{target: {name, vatNumber, address}, processingDate}`, and `GET …/lookup/{targetVrn}/{requesterVrn}` returning the same plus `requester` and `consultationNumber`. `Address` **requires** `line1`, `postcode` and `countryCode`. Errors are `INVALID_REQUEST` (400), `NOT_FOUND` (404, *"targetVrn does not match a registered company"*) and `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` (500).

**Ruled now, so the eventual brief has an authority and not a guess:**

1. **The unverified route only. The verified route is barred.** It requires `requesterVrn` — **the caller's own VAT registration number** — and returns a `consultationNumber` that is evidence *that VAT registrant* performed the check. registry-mcp is a proxy: the only VRN it could send is the operator's, which would make every consultation number in the system attest that the operator checked, on behalf of a caller it cannot name. That is a false record generated at scale, and it is worse than useless to the caller who actually wanted the proof. `include=` never accepts a second identifier on this path (D-042(b) passes attachments an `id`, singular), so the barred route is also the one the mechanism cannot express — which is a good sign rather than a coincidence.
2. **A third credential shape, and it is not `include_api_key_env`'s.** D-037 met two secrets for one registry and solved it with one pointer plus a complete hint; D-045(c) met one secret per attachment and added `include_api_key_env`. This is **two secrets for one attachment** — a client id and a client secret exchanged for a bearer token — so `include_api_key_env`'s `Mapping[str, str]` cannot express it without becoming a tuple, which D-037 declined by name. **The fix is D-037's, reused verbatim: the mapping's value names `HMRC_VAT_CLIENT_ID`, and its hint names both variables.** One pointer, complete hint, no widened contract, and a token cache is an implementation detail behind the client and never a `CountryInfo` field.
3. **The block carries the registered name and address of a *business*, and for a GB sole trader that is a person's — so the address is dropped and the name is bound.** `id_may_be_personal` is `False` for GB and stays `False`: it describes the **identifier** (D-040(a)), and neither a CRN nor a VRN is a natural person's identifier. That flag therefore does **not** protect this block, and nothing else in the codebase does either. HMRC registers sole traders for VAT under their own name, `Address.line1` and `postcode` are **required** fields in HMRC's schema, and for a sole trader that address is frequently residential. So:
   * **`name` is relayed and is bound by D-028(1)** — never a lookup key, never an index, never searchable, never in a log (D-040) — and joins `Charge.parties_entitled` and `ParentLink.legal_name` in `legal/terms.md`'s list. It cannot be dropped: the whole point of the check is that the name on the invoice matches the name on the VAT register.
   * **The address is *not* relayed as an address.** No `Address` model, no `line1`, no street. What is carried is **`postcode_area`, the outward code only** (`SW97 5CK` → `SW97`) — enough for the one thing a caller does with it, which is *is this registrant plausibly where the invoice says they are*, and short of a residence. D-045(h)'s Norwegian rule is the precedent and it is stated there as a principle rather than a Norwegian quirk: *"a full `fødselsdato` beside a full name is materially closer to a national identifier than the UK's month-and-year, and the field is truncated to year-month at the boundary or dropped."* Truncate at the boundary, in the client, before the value reaches a model — not in the surface, and never conditionally on legal form, because HMRC's response does not say whether the registrant is a sole trader and D-040(c),(d) refuse to protect a question by inspecting its shape.
   * *Declined: relaying the full address for companies and truncating only for sole traders.* We cannot tell them apart from this payload, and a rule that depends on a distinction the wire does not make is the per-field split in someone else's data that D-045(d) refused for the Gazette.
4. **`vat_registered` stays where D-045(b) left it.** This block does not become a source for `CompanyReport.vat_registered`: it is keyed by **VAT number**, not by company identifier, and a lookup that starts from a VRN cannot fill a field on a report fetched by CRN without a join nobody has ruled. Whether this is an attachment at all or a different operation is the first question its brief must answer, and it is **not** answered here.
5. **No brief today.** The credential is two weeks away and the terms are accepted at registration; writing a brief against unaccepted terms is D-045(c)'s objection in a second costume. This part exists so that when the credential arrives, the block's shape is a decision already made rather than an implementer's improvisation under time pressure.

**(f) `tasks/T52-recon.md` read both registers' filed accounts on 2026-09-09, and D-043(i) is wrong in both directions. Sweden's absence was our scope decision and the measurement reverses it; Britain's is the register's own population and must be recorded as such. Two open questions the recon leaves to the architect are ruled here.**

**Everything in this part is `tasks/T52-recon.md`'s, fetched that day** (commit `a2faccd`, on `main` and not in this worktree). Nothing here was re-fetched and nothing rests on a claim it marked `[search]` without a `[fetched]` corroboration beside it.

D-043(i) says Britain's and Sweden's figures are alike blocked by *"a scope decision we made"*. **Sweden's were, and D-041(g)'s three reasons do not survive contact with the document.** D-041(g) ruled Bolagsverket's zip out as *"a third request, an XBRL package to parse, and bytes we would have to interpret"*. Measured: it is a **second** request, not a third — `filings` already makes the `/dokumentlista` call and `financials` shares it; the "package" is a **45 KB zip containing exactly one file**, a 123–157 KB inline-XBRL XHTML, not the iXBRL + XBRL + PDF triple the submission documentation describes; and the bytes parse in **2.3–4.0 ms with the standard library** (`zipfile`, `xml.etree`), no new runtime dependency, across **zero dimensioned contexts** in any of the three documents sampled. On the substance: **15–16 of the 19 D-043(c) figures present on every document**, including `total_assets` and `total_equity_and_liabilities`; one concept name per figure, **byte-identical across a 2017-09-30 and a 2021-10-31 taxonomy five years apart**; correct signs on losses; and **18 of 18 of the documents' own balance-sheet identities reconciling exactly**. This is D-043(a)'s own move made a second time — D-043(a) held that D-041(g)'s bar was *parsing filed documents* and that the Norwegian key figures cleared it by arriving parsed; here the bar is met head-on, and what the measurement shows is that it was set against a document nobody had opened. **The scope decision is reversed for Sweden, `include=["financials"]` is ruled in for `SE`, and `tasks/T55.md` is written (g).**

**Britain's is not a scope decision and saying so is the load-bearing half of this part.** Three findings, in the order that decides it, none of them about parsing. **(1) The companies a caller asks about do not have the document** — Tesco 25 of 25 accounts filings PDF-only, NatWest Markets 25 of 25, Monzo and Deloitte LLP the same; the register's own ratio is ~75% electronic and the missing quarter is the large-filer end. A block that is `null` for every company anyone has heard of is worse than no block, because it teaches a caller that the capability does not work. **(2) The figures are not in the document even when the document exists** — a micro-entity filing carries 5 of ~20 D-043(c) fields and no income statement at all, the best case measured was 8 of 19, and **no British filing sampled carried `total_assets`, `liabilities` or `total_equity_and_liabilities`**, because the FRS balance-sheet format has no such lines and D-043(e) forbids adding two numbers to make one. **(3) The sign is in the presentation, not in the fact, and the failure is silent.** The recon's Fact 6 is the sharpest thing in it: a well-formed `uk-core:NetAssetsLiabilities` fact, in GBP, on the right context, agreeing with the same document's own `Equity` and `TotalAssetsLessCurrentLiabilities` — three tagged facts corroborating each other — is wrong by twice its value with its sign inverted, on a company in compulsory liquidation. Every internal check an implementer would think to write passes; `ixbrlparse` returns the same number; Companies House accepted the filing. **A block that reports a solvent balance sheet for an insolvent company on a credit question is the worst failure available in this product**, and it is reachable by code that looks right.

So: **D-042(g)'s "record it as the register's silence" case, not D-043(i)'s "the reason is ours".** *Declined: building GB `financials` behind a validity cross-check.* The check that catches Fact 6 (`FixedAssets + CurrentAssets − Creditors[WithinOneYear]` against the tagged `TotalAssetsLessCurrentLiabilities`) works, and its honest output when it fails is **no `BalanceSheet` at all plus a note** — because nothing identifies which of the four facts is mistagged — so the feature would be a block that is absent for the 25% who file PDFs, empty for the micro-entities, partial for the rest, and silently withheld whenever the cross-check trips. That is four states of nothing. **Revisit after 1 April 2028 on evidence of what the register actually publishes, and not on the strength of the legislation**, because the same instrument that makes iXBRL universal gives small and micro entities *"the option to opt out of having these profit and loss accounts published on the public register"* — so the population that gains machine-readable accounts is precisely the population entitled to withhold the two numbers a credit question wants. `include=["financials"]` for `GB` stays `bad_request` naming GB's allowed set (D-042(d)(3)), and the reason recorded in `UK_SPEC.md` is the population, never the parser.

**Open question 1 — how many periods a Swedish `financials` block carries. Ruled: one, the most recent, and no argument buys more.** The recon is right that D-043 has no precedent to inherit: Norway carries one period because Regnskapsregisteret returns one in a body we already have, which is a register limit; Sweden would carry one because we choose it, which is ours. Four reasons, and the third is the one that settles it.

1. **`FinancialSummary.periods` being a list is not an instruction to fill it.** It is plural so that a register which publishes a series can carry one, and D-043(c) shipped it plural against a register that publishes one.
2. **The cost model is one `/dokument` request per period.** The single production Swedish company with documents (`5561890038`) lists **six**, 2020-12-31 through 2025-12-31. Six periods is six requests plus the shared list call, against Bolagsverket's **60-per-minute budget shared by every call including the token refresh** — an attachment that costs two requests for one caller and seven for another is an unbounded fan-out on a metered budget, which is what D-041(b) refused for the batch path and what D-042(j)'s truncation rule exists to bound.
3. **A trend is a different question, and the sibling block already answers the part of it we can afford.** D-042(c) one level down: *"is it filing on time"* and *"what do the numbers say"* were split by D-043(b); *"what do this year's numbers say"* and *"how have they moved over six years"* split the same way, and the second is a series product rather than a register relay. `include=["filings"]` already carries every document's date, period end and `dokumentId` in the **same** `/dokumentlista` body, at no extra cost — so a caller asking for both gets the full list of what exists beside the parsed latest, which is the honest shape.
4. **Truncation is disclosed, never silent** (D-042(j)). The block's `notes` must say how many annual reports the list reported, which period was parsed, and that the rest are listed in `filings` — the same disclose-the-discovery-step move D-046(d) made for `smp_url`.

*Declined: all periods.* *Declined: a `periods=` or `years=` parameter on the attachment.* D-042(b) passes an attachment an `id` and nothing else; a per-attachment parameter gives one mechanism two contracts, which is the shape D-045(g) declined for `lookup_with`'s `allowed=`. *Declined: the most recent **two**, for a year-on-year comparison.* Two is a number with no argument behind it, and the comparison it enables is the derived figure D-043(e) refuses.

**Open question 2 — the cache. Ruled: three keys, one new kind, and the document itself is never stored anywhere.**

* **`SE:bolagsverket:filings:{env}:{id}` — the `/dokumentlista` body, shared, unchanged.** `filings` and `financials` make **one** list call between them, one key per upstream call (D-043(h)(1)), existing TTL. And the same race D-043(h)(3) named and D-047(a) hit again is here for the third time: two attachment methods, bounded concurrency, one cold key. One shared in-flight result, asserted in a test.
* **`SE:bolagsverket:financials:{env}:{dokumentId}` — the parsed figures, keyed on the document and not on the company.** This is the ruling the recon asked for and it follows from one fact: **an annual report is immutable once filed.** A correction is a new filing with a new `dokumentId`, so a stale hit on this key cannot be a wrong number — it can only be a document that is no longer the latest, and *which* document is latest is decided by the list key, which has its own short TTL. New kind `financials` in D-045(e)'s per-kind table (no collision: Norway's key is `NO:brreg:accounts:{orgnr}`, kind `accounts`), **`(30 * 24 * 3600, 3600)`**. *Declined: caching it indefinitely,* which "immutable" invites. Nothing in this codebase is cached forever, the store has no eviction policy of its own, and a 30-day ceiling costs one re-parse a month for a document that will not have changed — the cheapest possible insurance against a taxonomy handling bug outliving its fix. The empty half of the pair is nominal: a `/dokument` fetch returns a document or fails, so `status="not_found"` does not arise on this key, and the pair is declared in full anyway so the table has no special case.
* **The document is parsed and discarded in the same coroutine. Nothing writes the zip, the XHTML, or any `ix:nonNumeric` value to the cache, to a log, or to disk.** This is a hard rule and not a footprint preference. Every person-bearing fact in these documents is an `ix:nonNumeric` — measured: **346 numeric facts across six documents, 0 of which is a name** — and the signature concepts are exactly that (`se-gen-base:UnderskriftArsredovisningForetradareTilltalsnamn` / `…Efternamn` on the 2017 taxonomy, **renamed** to `se-gen-base:UnderskriftHandlingTilltalsnamn` / `…Efternamn` on the 2021 one). A cached iXBRL blob is a store of personal data with a retention policy nobody has written, which is precisely what D-028(2)'s clamp exists to prevent, and it would arrive here without anyone deciding to build it. The parsed `FinancialPeriod` carries none of it.

**And one rule that outlives Britain's exclusion, so it is not re-derived when 2028 comes: `SourceRef.license` on a figure derived from a filed document never names OGL, in either country.** Companies House's Crown-copyright grant carves out third-party copyright by name — *"The permission to reproduce Crown protected material does not extend to any material on this site which is identified as being the copyright of a third party"* — and a set of filed accounts is authored by the company and its accountants, not by the Crown; the *"All content is available under the Open Government Licence v3.0"* string is a footer on a campaign site, not a statement about the register's document store. Sweden's licence value is D-038's existing Bolagsverket sentence, unchanged and already a described regime rather than a name, and the personal-data proviso inside it (*"så länge användningen inte bryter mot lagar om skydd av personuppgifter eller sekretess"*) is answered by the `ix:nonFraction`-only rule rather than by an assurance.

**(g) `tasks/T55.md` is written** for a Sonnet: `registries/se/financials.py` plus the parser in `registries/se/` — **one filler is not a shared abstraction**, D-044(a)'s *measure first, then unify*, and the only candidate second caller is the country (f) has just closed. The models are D-043(c)'s `FinancialSummary` / `FinancialPeriod` / `IncomeStatement` / `BalanceSheet` **field-identically, with no new field**: D-042(g) and D-044(a) both bind, the Norwegian block is canonical, and a Swedish variant of a shared model is the country-scoped payload D-042(g) declined by name. Four things the brief makes non-negotiable, each because the recon measured the failure: **`currency` comes from the unit** (`unitRef` → `xbrli:measure` → `iso4217:SEK`) and never from `se-cd-base:Redovisningsvaluta`, which was renamed *and* re-typed between the two taxonomy versions — D-043(d) makes `currency` required with no default, so a parser keyed on the concept builds nothing at all on the current taxonomy; **the extractor reads `ix:nonFraction` and never `ix:nonNumeric`**, asserted as a static test of the element filter rather than as a concept blocklist, because Sweden renamed its signature concepts between the two versions sampled and a blocklist would have gone stale silently; **`accounting_framework` is `"K2"` / `"K3"` from the `link:schemaRef` href path**, not from a concept; and **no ratio is read, computed or relayed** — the register itself tags `se-gen-base:Soliditet`, and the same company filed the identical quantity as `−0.1761` in one report and `−17.61` in another's comparison column, both under `unitRef="procent"` resolving to `xbrli:measure = pure`, which nothing in the document disambiguates. That is D-043(e)'s refusal of derived indicators handed the strongest possible evidence: here the register *offers* the ratio and it is unusable.

**The first step of T55 is a recon of one document, and it is in the brief because the recon named it as the gap it could not close.** All three Swedish documents sampled are the same company under **K2**; **no K3 document has been seen at all**, and `se-gen-base` being the shared base of both is a `[search]` expectation rather than a measurement. So T55 opens with **one `/dokumentlista` and one `/dokument` on a K3 aktiebolag**, recording whether the concept names hold; if they do not, it stops and reports rather than shipping a parser that works on one taxonomy profile. The done-check is the recon's, verbatim: `5561890038`'s 2025 report yields `total_assets == total_equity_and_liabilities == 515409.0`, `equity == 508409.0`, `profit_for_period == -10536.0`, `currency == "SEK"`.

**And `registries/se/filings.py`'s scope note can now say what it could not.** T44 shipped `_SCOPE_NOTE` saying the list *"may omit companies that do not file digitally"*; the recon establishes the population — **digitally submitted aktiebolag annual reports under K2 or K3, from 2020** — and explains the fact T44 recorded without being able to account for: Ericsson (`5560160680`) returns a present `filings` block with an empty document list not because it files on paper but because **an IFRS preparer cannot use the channel at all**. T55 corrects that sentence in the same footprint. The Swedish mandate is **a bill and not a law** and must not be stated as one anywhere.
Reason: three of the first five parts came out narrower than the ranking that prompted them, and in every case the narrowing was done by counting rather than by arguing. The parents block survives a 97%-miss rate because the miss is *free* — GLEIF puts the two states in the Level 1 body, which nobody had noticed, so the expensive half of the feature is only paid on the 3% of lookups where there is something to say — and it survives its own most common answer only because that answer can be shipped with the measurement that undermines it attached. Equinor is the case worth remembering: the single most-cited company in this repository publishes, through a CC0 source we already trust for its LEI, a machine-readable statement that it is controlled by natural persons, and a block that relayed the word without the paragraph would have told a caller that Norway's state oil company is privately owned. The other lesson is (b)'s, and it is a correction to a habit rather than to a fact: the FCA's most quotable licence sentence forbids exactly what this product does, and it belongs to a different service — reading one document further is what stopped a source being refused on somebody else's terms, which is the mirror image of the mistake D-045(b) and (d) caught by reading one document further in the other direction. (f) and (g) are the same discipline arriving from a different direction and are worth reading beside D-043(a): a bar this file set in D-041(g) — *a third request, an XBRL package to parse* — turns out, when somebody finally opens the package, to have been set against a 45 KB single-file zip that parses in four milliseconds of standard library, and the country it was blocking is the one where every balance-sheet total is present and every identity reconciles. The reverse is also true and is the reason (f) spends its length on Britain: the country that looked like a parsing problem is a **population** problem, the parser that would solve it works, and the number it returns on a real filing is inverted, corroborated by three other tagged facts, and about a company in compulsory liquidation. Both halves say the same thing — a scope decision made from a document's description and a scope decision made from the document are different decisions, and only one of them is evidence.
Applies to tasks: **`tasks/T53.md`** (GLEIF Level 2, this entry, (a)) and **`tasks/T54.md`** (the FCA, (b), gated on `HUMAN_TODO.md` §7.10 item 1); `HUMAN_TODO.md` **§7.10** (d), written by this task; the surfaces-sync task's prose list **grows by `parents` and, when T54 lands, `regulator`** — the words *parent*, *group*, *ultimate parent*, *GLEIF*, *FCA*, *authorised*, *regulated* and *appointed representative* must reach `lookup_company`'s description text under D-042(d)(2), and no implementer writes them; **amends D-045(e)** (`universal_includes` becomes `{"lei", "parents"}`; the per-kind TTL table gains `"parents": (7 days, 24 h)`); **sharpens D-042(c)** (a publisher's own product split is evidence for the distinct-question rule) and **D-046(d)** (one `SourceRef` for a bounded fan-out; each leg's URL disclosed as a field); **extends D-042(e)(3)/D-045(a)**'s D-028(1) binding to `ParentLink.legal_name` and, prospectively, to an HMRC VAT `name`, so `legal/terms.md`'s list goes from three fields to four; **restates D-045(c)**'s `include_api_key_env` unamended and adds D-037's one-pointer-plus-complete-hint shape for the two-secret attachment (e)(2); research item 15 is **closed as declined** (c); **`tasks/T55.md`** (Sweden's `financials`, (f) and (g)) and `registries/se/filings.py`'s scope note, which T55 corrects in the same footprint; **amends D-043(i)** (Sweden's absence was ours and is reversed, Britain's is the register's population and is recorded as such) and **reverses D-041(g)** for Bolagsverket's `/dokument` zip on measurement; **adds `"financials": (30 days, 1 h)`** to D-045(e)'s table beside `parents`; closes `tasks/T52-recon.md`'s two open questions (periods: one, the most recent; cache: keyed on `dokumentId`, and the document itself is never stored); `CORE_ROADMAP_SPEC.md` §1's order gains a Swedish `financials` item and **GB `financials` is closed until after 1 April 2028, on evidence rather than on legislation**; **D-024, D-028 and D-031 unamended, and the published tool count does not move**

### D-048 — The token diet: the tool surface is a document a caller pays for on every conversation, so each thing is said once, in the place it is read; `null` keys and the two connector aliases survive the audit and are ruled permanent; and a budget test stops the surface growing back
Date: 2026-09-09
Decision: Kim, 2026-09-09: *"I want to make the costs of running low (as low as possible) without sacrificing quality."* The server runs no model, every upstream is free and hosting is at Railway's floor, so the only cost this project imposes that it can still lower is **the caller's context**: the tool list plus `instructions`, paid in full once per conversation and at a tenth thereafter under prompt caching.

**The measurement, and a correction to the brief's number.** All figures below are `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` on `claude-opus-5`, taken on 2026-09-09 against this worktree (`claude-sonnet-5` counts ~0.7% higher and ranks identically; `claude-fable-5-1` is identical to Opus). `tasks/T59.md` quotes **9,789**; that was measured before **T60** merged, and T60 named Sweden in `_COUNTRY_DESCRIPTION` and `_ID_DESCRIPTION` — five strings on the wire, +335 tokens. **The baseline this entry rules against is `main` at `10b5712`: 9,274 (seven tools) + 850 (`instructions`) = 10,124.** Every "before" number here is from that tree.

Where it goes, measured: `lookup_company`'s description 2,257 · `_INCLUDE_DESCRIPTION` 647 · `company_deadlines`' description 1,078 · `search_company`'s 797 · `validate_company_id`'s 705 · `list_countries`' 231 · `search` 142 + `fetch` 143 · `_ID_DESCRIPTION` 196 **× 3 tools** = 588 · `_COUNTRY_DESCRIPTION` 125 **× 4 tools** = 500 · `_DEADLINE_INCLUDE_DESCRIPTION` 229 · `instructions` 850. The irreducible floor — every name, every input schema, every `examples` array, zero prose — is **1,196**, so the prose is about 88% of the bill and is where every ruling below acts.

**The governing rule, from which (a), (b) and (c) all follow: a fact is written once, on the surface the model reads when it needs that fact, and nowhere else.** The tool list is not documentation, it is a document the caller is billed for, and the same paragraph in three tools is billed three times. What this entry does **not** do is trade a fact for tokens: every word D-042(d)(2), D-043(b), D-045(e), D-046 and D-047 put on this surface is still on it, and (h) makes that mechanical.

**(a) The attachments are explained once, in the `include` argument, and named once, in the description. −850 tokens.** D-042(d)(2) requires the block names and the retrieval words to reach `lookup_company`'s *description*, because under tool search the name and first sentence are the retrieval key; it does not require the *explanations* to be there. Today they are in both: the description's two attachment paragraphs cost **1,296** and `_INCLUDE_DESCRIPTION` costs **647**, and between them the seven blocks are described twice.

The split is by **which question the string answers**. *"Is there a tool that can tell me about charges?"* is answered before the model has chosen a tool, so the block names and their retrieval words stay in the description — one paragraph, **246 tokens**, naming `filings`, `charges`, `insolvency`, `financials`, `lei`, `parents`, `peppol` with the words *filing history, mortgages, security interests, winding-up, administration, annual accounts, turnover, operating result, profit, balance sheet, solvency, GLEIF, Legal Entity Identifier, ultimate parent, group, e-invoice, EHF, 1 January 2027*. *"What exactly does `charges` return, and can this country give it to me?"* is asked only while filling in arguments, so the per-block detail — the country scope, the two-level nullability, and **every caveat D-046, D-047 and T58's must-land fix put there** — lives in `_INCLUDE_DESCRIPTION`, one clause per block, **847 tokens**. 1,943 → 1,093.

The caveats that must survive the move verbatim in substance, named here so a reviewer can check them one by one: a members' voluntary liquidation is a *solvent* wind-up (`is_liquidation: true` is not distress); `financials` absent vs. empty vs. `bad_request` across Sweden, Norway and Britain (T58, five surfaces); `lei: null` in a present block means GLEIF holds none, and Sweden is excluded because its identifier can be a natural person's (D-045(e), D-047(a)); a `parents` exception is a **category word, never a name**, and is unverified (D-047(a)); `peppol`'s `registered: null` is never "no", and only an NXDOMAIN or an SMP 404 earns `false` (D-046). `_INCLUDE_DESCRIPTION` is now the single place any of these is written on the tool surface, which is why (h) pins them there.

*Declined: dropping the block names from the description and relying on the schema enum.* D-042(c)'s escape hatch exists because "an enum value inside an input schema is not a retrieval key", and (d)(2) is the whole of what pays for `include=[…]` not being seven tools. Reversing it to save 246 tokens would spend the thing the depth track was built on.

**(b) The D-007 contract is written once, in `instructions`; each tool keeps a pointer plus only what is true of *that* tool. −243 tokens.** Today the same paragraph is in `lookup_company` (295), `search_company` (182), `company_deadlines` (173) and, shortened, `validate_company_id` (60), with a fourth copy of its first sentence in `instructions` (51) — 761 for one contract. `instructions` now carries it in full, code by code (210): `invalid_id` → fix or `search_company`, never retry the same string; `not_found` → `search_company`; `unsupported_country` → `list_countries`; `bad_request` → read the hint, which names the allowed value; `not_implemented` → change approach, not timing; `rate_limited` → slow down; `upstream_error`/`upstream_timeout` → already retried once here, wait about a minute.
