Each tool keeps one sentence pointing there, plus its own non-generic facts — which is why the four remaining sentences cost 104/71/79/54 rather than 30 each: `lookup_company`'s also carries **a failed attachment is not an error** (D-042(j)), `company_deadlines`' that any `lookup_company` code can surface through it, `validate_company_id`'s that it **never raises for a malformed identifier** (D-010).

**The eval that tests error recovery does not depend on any of the removed words, and this is checkable rather than hopeful.** `E16`'s structured check is `error.hint` *contains* `search_company` — that is `RegistryError.hint`, built in `registries/no/`, untouched by this entry; its `--agent` check is that the answer does not claim a company was found. `E26`'s `--agent` check is `forbidden_tools: [lookup_company]`, which depends on the surface still telling a model to call `list_countries` when a country is in doubt — so **that pointer stays in all four tool descriptions and in `instructions`**, and (h) pins it. The load-bearing mechanism was never the description: D-007 makes `hint` mandatory and non-empty on every raised error and requires it to name the next call, so the contract travels with the failure to every client, including one that renders no `instructions` at all. The descriptions were belt over braces, three times.

**(c) The country explanations are written once — the identifier shapes on the `id` argument and in `list_countries`, the register names on `lookup_company` — and the keyword rule is untouched. −1,017 tokens.** `KEYWORDS.md` §2 requires rows 1–9, GB-1…GB-7 and SE-1…SE-11 in the per-country tools' first two sentences, and that rule **stays**: the aliases are how an agent finds this server at all. What goes is the *explanation* wrapped around them, repeated four times.

* `_ID_DESCRIPTION` 196 → 115, **× 3 tools: 588 → 345.** Keeps all three countries' identifier names (T60), the normalisation facts (spaces, dots, hyphens, `NO...MVA`, a short CRN), and points at `list_countries` for the exact shape.
* `_COUNTRY_DESCRIPTION` 125 → 64, **× 4 tools: 500 → 256.** Keeps the three codes with their country names (T60) and D-015's `UK` rejection; drops the register names, which are on `lookup_company` and in `list_countries`' own payload.
* The four docstrings' country paragraphs 1,466 → 1,066, and `instructions`' 427 → 297. `company_deadlines` keeps its statutory obligation list (*årsregnskap, generalforsamling, skattemelding, aksjonærregisteroppgaven, mva-melding, a-melding*; *annual accounts, confirmation statement (CS01)*; *ordinarie bolagsstämma / årsstämma, årsredovisning, förseningsavgift*) because that list **is** this tool's answer, not a country recital, and it is the retrieval key for the product's differentiator. `validate_company_id` keeps its normalisation worked examples for the same reason: they are what the tool does.
* **What the shortened strings point at already exists and is already served.** `registry://rules/{country}` carries every country's identifier rules, check-digit algorithm, roll-forward treatment, legal sources and licence, in full — and `applies_because` carries the same reasoning at *runtime*, in the response, for the specific company asked about. The tool description's job is to get the model to the right tool and the right argument; it is not the rules document, and the rules document is not hypothetical.

**(d) `search` and `fetch` stay, for every client, at the D-031(e) minimum. Ruled permanent. Measured cost of the pair: 589 tokens.** The brief estimated ≈1,180; that double-counts the per-tool framing the isolated measurement adds. **Measured properly: the seven-tool list costs 9,274 and the five-tool list costs 8,665, so the aliases cost 609 today and 589 after this entry** — 8% of the fixed cost, for the only calling convention by which ChatGPT deep research can reach this server at all.

**The `clientInfo` question is settled on evidence, and the answer is no, twice over.** The brief asked whether FastMCP 4.0.2 can serve the aliases only to a client that names itself ChatGPT. *It can*, and this was verified rather than assumed: `tools/list` is dispatched inside `bind_request_context(ctx)` (`fastmcp/server/mixins/mcp_operations.py:139-141`), which sets the `fastmcp_request_ctx` ContextVar (`fastmcp/server/dependencies.py:119-131`), so `Context.request_context` is live and `Context.session` returns the request's `ServerSession` (`fastmcp/server/context.py:794-812`) — the same path `client_supports_extension` already uses at `fastmcp/server/low_level.py:123`. A probe middleware reading `ctx.session.client_params.client_info.name` inside `on_list_tools` saw `'openai-mcp'`, `'claude-code'` and `'cursor'` correctly and returned a *different tool list* to the first. So the mechanism is real.

It is still refused, and D-031(b) — *"the protocol offers no negotiated tool profile; a server can only guess, and we do not"* — is **reaffirmed, not amended**, with two facts D-031 could not have had. First, **the field is being removed from the protocol**: `InitializeRequestParams` is documented in the installed SDK as *"Removed in protocol 2026-07-28; sent/received on sessions negotiating <= 2025-11-25. On 2026-07-28 the handshake is `server/discover` plus per-request `_meta`"* (`mcp_types/_types.py:515-524`), and the in-memory client here negotiates **2026-07-28** — the version at which it is gone. Second, on the replacement path **client identity is optional by specification**: `Connection.from_envelope` synthesises `client_params` *"when client info was supplied too"* and otherwise leaves it `None`, degrading a mis-shaped value "to not-supplied rather than failing the request", because "client info is optional per spec PR #3002" (`mcp/server/connection.py:276-296`). A tool list keyed on that field is keyed on something a conforming client need not send and a future one will not — it would fail *open or closed* silently, and the directory-scraped list would still not be the list a user gets. Against 589 tokens, that is not a trade; it is a defect bought at a discount.

So the aliases stay for everyone and their prose shrinks to D-031(e)'s stated minimum: the pinned first sentence (which `tests/test_connector.py::test_connector_descriptions_open_by_naming_the_preferred_tool` already asserts, and which must keep asserting) plus one clause of substance. 285 → 267, and their argument descriptions with them. **This paragraph is the ruling of record: nobody re-opens client-conditional tool lists without an amendment here.**

**(e) `filings` keeps its 25-document page. No default page, and the reason is a correctness one.** 25 × 116 = 3,516 tokens is the largest single block, and a smaller default is tempting — but `FilingHistory.financial_year_end` is *"the latest reporting period among this entity's filed annual accounts"* **on the page returned**, and `registries/se/rules.py:737-739` reads exactly that field to replace Sweden's assumed 31 December year end with the register's own figure. A shorter page returns `None` more often, and `None` puts the Swedish annual-report deadline back on the assumption T44 spent a whole task removing — a rung-2 date silently demoted to rung 3 to save tokens the caller *asked for*. The honesty machinery is already there and stays: `total_count` says how many more the register holds and `notes` says so in words (`core/models.py:1296-1345`). **`charges` is unchanged, as D-045(a) ruled: the free text is the answer.** The real lever on per-call cost is telling a caller what a block costs *before* they ask, which is why `static/llms-full.txt` gains the per-attachment token table in Part 2.

**(f) `null` keys stay. Ruled, so it is not proposed again.** Stripping them saves 7–30% of every response (Tesco base 931 → 661; the full pull 8,496 → 6,791) and is the single largest number in this entry. It is refused because **`null` is a value in this contract, not the absence of one.** D-011 (`employees_reported`), D-026(b)/(c), D-028(3), D-041(d), D-042(d)(3) and D-043(b) all rest on present-`null` meaning *"the register was asked and does not say"* while an **absent key** means *"this was not fetched"* — the two-states-into-one collapse this file has now refused six times. Strip the nulls and every one of those distinctions is destroyed at the serialiser, on the wire, for a caller who has no way to tell which happened. `extra="forbid"` and the REST≡MCP byte-identity tests (D-004) would also both have to go. The `register_coverage` prompt exists to teach an agent to read a `null` correctly; a 27% saving bought by making that prompt a lie is not a saving. **No opt-in flag either** — two wire shapes for one operation is the drift D-004 exists to prevent, and the cheap version of this idea is (e)'s: let the caller not ask for the block.

**(g) `SourceRef.fetched_at` keeps its microseconds. Nothing changes. Measured: 3 tokens.** A `SourceRef` with microseconds counts 83; at second precision, 80 — 3 tokens, ×6 on a full pull, 18 of 8,496 (0.2%). Against that: `fetched_at` is set from `datetime.now(UTC)` in `core/`, `registries/no/`, `registries/gb/` and `registries/se/` (nine call sites), it is preserved verbatim across cache hits by D-006 so that staleness is visible, and it is compared byte-for-byte by the REST≡MCP parity tests and by committed fixtures. Truncating it is a four-module change with a fixture re-record, in the one field whose whole job is to be exact, for 0.2%. **Refused**, and it is the clearest case in this entry of a saving that is not worth its blast radius.

**(h) A budget test, in `tests/test_mcp.py`, counting with a local proxy — and a word-pinning test that gets stronger, never weaker.** CI has no API key, so the budget is counted as **characters ÷ 2.60**, a ratio measured here twice on the real thing (2.612 before the diet, 2.606 after — the two figures bracket it, and both are recorded in the test as its provenance). The counted blob is exactly what the wire carries: `json.dumps` of the seven tools' `name` + `description` + `inputSchema`, plus `instructions`. **N = 8,100 estimated tokens**, which is the post-diet measurement (7,402) plus 10%, and the test also asserts a raw-character ceiling so that re-measuring the ratio cannot silently loosen the gate.

The word-pinning test is the other half, and it is what makes "no quality sacrificed" checkable rather than asserted. Today's surface carries **58 terms** from `KEYWORDS.md` §1/§GB/§SE and from the rulings that put words here — 54 of them on `lookup_company` (description plus its three argument descriptions), and `company registry`, `uk company search`, `confirmation statement` and `årsredovisning` elsewhere. The test pins **all 58, in those two groups, after normalising whitespace**. Whitespace normalisation is not a convenience: `uk company search` is today broken across a line in `search_company`'s docstring and so is *not a contiguous string on the wire* — a naive substring test would fail on text that is in fact present, and the Sonnet is about to reflow every paragraph in the file. The list may only ever grow.

**(i) The quality gate for Part 2, named exactly — and the brief's numbers are restated from a measured run.** `tasks/T59.md` says golden "23 pass / 3 skip"; that was the 26-case set, before group G's five connector cases landed. **Measured on this worktree today: `uv run python evals/run.py --golden` is 28 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped out of 31** (`E01`/`E02` live, `E25` no server interaction), and *that* is the number that must be unchanged. For `--agent`: `evals/reports/2026-09-05-agent-run-2.md` is 23/1/2 on `claude-sonnet-5` over 26 cases, so it is not comparable case-for-case with today's 31. The gate is therefore **a paired run on the same model (`claude-sonnet-5`, the harness default) in the same session — one before the edits and one after, both committed under `evals/reports/` — and no case that passed before may fail after.** **`E19` stays hard**: its `vat_registered is_null` fabrication gate and its `required_tools: [lookup_company]` stay exactly as they are. It already fails in `--agent` mode, reproducibly and for a documented reason (the model answers correctly from parametric knowledge without calling the tool), and it is kept failing on purpose — loosening it to make a diet look clean would retire the one case that measures whether this server is being *used*.

**Scope.** Prose only. No model, no field, no enum member, no route, no `core/`, no `registries/`, no wire byte of any response changes; the REST≡MCP parity tests and every fixture must pass untouched. `api/main.py`'s route descriptions mirror the same three rulings so the crawled OpenAPI does not drift from the tool surface (T46's rule), and the card is regenerated from the live server after every edit.

**The number.** Fixed cost **10,124 → 7,402**, measured: −2,722, **26.9% off the current baseline** and 24.4% off the pre-T60 figure `tasks/T59.md` quotes. The done-check's "at least 25% under 9,789" (≤ 7,342) is **not** reachable without cutting substance — the remaining 60 tokens would have to come out of the `include` caveats or the Norwegian deadline list — and 25% under the *measured* baseline (≤ 7,593) is beaten by 191. **The ruling is 7,402 measured, budget 8,100**, and the shortfall against the brief's arithmetic is recorded here rather than closed by shaving a caveat.
Reason: the one cost this project controls is somebody else's context window, and it had been paid three and four times over for the same sentences — not by carelessness but because each ruling that added a word added it wherever it might be read, and nothing ever went back to ask where it *is* read. The saving is therefore almost entirely deduplication, which is why it costs no facts; the two places a real fact was on offer for real tokens — `null` keys at 27% of every response, and a shorter `filings` page at 3,516 — are both refused, and refused on the same ground the rest of this file has refused five times before: a distinction that costs tokens is still a distinction, and a caller who cannot tell "the register does not say" from "we did not look" has been sold a cheaper answer to a different question. The `clientInfo` finding is the entry's one piece of luck: the mechanism D-031(b) declined on principle turns out to have been removed from the protocol four months later, so the principled answer and the durable one are the same answer.
Applies to tasks: **`tasks/T59.md` Part 2** (this entry, written into that file as the Sonnet's exact list); ships as **0.4.1**; reaffirms D-031(b) unamended and holds D-024's count, D-042(c)'s tool rule, D-042(d)(2)'s keyword requirement, D-045(a), D-046 and D-047 unchanged; amends nothing in this file.
